\bigoplus_{i\leq s}{\rm R}\Gamma(X,\Omega^{i})(s-i)[-i]\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{s+1}\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{s}

and
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{1}\simeq{\rm R}\Gamma(X,{\mathscr{O}}_{X})
by
[
18
, 3.27]
.
Having that, the same arguments as in the case of proper varieties over
K
yield short exact sequence

(6.11)

0\to H^{r}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(s))\to(H^{r}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\otimes_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\bf B}_{{\rm st}}^{+})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{s}}\to H^{r}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{s})\to 0

Moreover, these arguments show that the canonical map

H^{r}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{s}\to H^{r}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{s})

is an isomorphism. This can be proved in the following way.
By (
6.11
), we have a surjection

(H^{r}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\otimes_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\bf B}_{{\rm st}}^{+})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{s}}\twoheadrightarrow H^{r}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{s}).

Since the above map
factors through the natural map

(6.12)

H^{r}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{s}\to H^{r}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{s})

that latter is surjective as well. But it is also injective. Indeed, we have the distinguished triangle

F^{s}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{s}

It yields the long exact sequence of cohomology groups

\to H^{r}(F^{s}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f_{r}}}{{\to}}H^{r}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\to H^{r}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{s})\to

Since
F^{s}H^{r}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})=\operatorname{Im}f_{r}
, the map in (
6.12
) is injective. We are done.

The two isomorphisms in our theorem follow now from Remark
5.14
, using the last part of Remark
5.16
(take
M=H^{r}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X),M^{+}=F^{0}(H^{r}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\otimes_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}}{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}})
).
∎

Remark 6.13
.

(i)
One can restate the theorem as follows (
{\mathscr{E}}(-,-)
is the associated vector bundle on

X_{\rm FF}
):

H^{r}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p})\simeq H^{0}(X_{\rm FF},{\mathscr{E}}(H^{r}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X),H^{r}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}))).

(ii) From Theorem
6.8
, we get natural isomorphisms

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}_{\rm VS}(H^{r}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}),{\mathbb{B}}_{\operatorname{st}})\simeq{\rm Hom}_{F^{\rm nr}}(H^{r}_{{\rm HK}}(X),{\bf B}_{{\rm st}}),\quad{\text{as ${\bf B}_{{\rm st}}$-modules,}}

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}_{\rm VS}(H^{r}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}),{\mathbb{B}}_{\operatorname{dR}})\simeq{\rm Hom}_{{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}}(H^{r}_{{\rm dR}}(X/{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}),{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}),\quad{\text{as ${\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}$-modules}}.

6.3.
Dagger Stein varieties and dagger affinoids

Having the comparison result from
[
18
, Cor. 6.9]
, we can now deduce a (simplified) fundamental diagram for pro-étale cohomology from the one for overconvergent syntomic cohomology.

Theorem 6.14
.

(Simplified fundamental diagram)

Let
X
be a smooth dagger Stein variety or a smooth dagger affinoid over
C
.
Let
r\geq 0
. There is a natural map of strictly exact sequences in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf Q}_{p}})

\textstyle{0\to\Omega^{r-1}(X)/\operatorname{Ker}d\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{r}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\beta}}
\textstyle{(H^{r}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\to 0}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}\otimes\theta}
\textstyle{0\to\Omega^{r-1}(X)/\operatorname{Ker}d\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d}
\textstyle{\Omega^{r}(X)^{d=0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{r}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\longrightarrow 0}

Moreover,

H^{r}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))
is Fréchet or LB, respectively,
the vertical maps are strict and have closed images, and

\operatorname{Ker}\tilde{\beta}\simeq(H^{r}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r-1}}
.

Proof.

We define
\tilde{\beta}:=p^{-r}\beta\alpha_{r}^{-1}
, using
[
18
, Cor. 6.9]
and
[
18
, Prop. 5.13]
; the twist by
p^{-r}
being added to make this map compatible with symbols.
The theorem follows immediately from
[
18
, Cor. 6.9]
and

[
18
, Prop. 5.13]
.
∎

6.4.
The fundamental diagram

We will now introduce the fundamental diagram, look at some examples, where it appears, and, finally, state a conjecture concerning it.

6.4.1.
Examples

We start with examples.

\bullet

Proper varieties.

Corollary 6.15
.

Let
X
be a smooth proper variety over
C
.
We have the bicartesian diagram

(6.16)

\textstyle{H^{r}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{\operatorname{st}}(r)}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{\operatorname{dR}}(r)}
\textstyle{(H^{r}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\otimes_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\bf B}_{{\rm st}}^{+})^{\varphi=p^{r},N=0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}\otimes\iota}
\textstyle{F^{r}H^{r}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{can}}
\textstyle{H^{r}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})}

Recall that this means that this is a pushout and pullback diagram, or, that the sequence

(6.17)

0\to H^{r}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\alpha_{\operatorname{st}}(r)\oplus\alpha_{\operatorname{dR}}(r)}}(H^{r}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\otimes_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\bf B}_{{\rm st}}^{+})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}\oplus F^{r}H^{r}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}\otimes\iota+\operatorname{can}}}H^{r}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\to 0

is exact.

Proof.

Follows immediately from the short exact sequence (
6.11
).
∎

Remark 6.18
.

(i) The passage
the other way, from diagram (
6.16
) to Theorem
6.8
is also possible: the exact sequence (
6.17
) yields the exact sequence (
6.11
) and we can finish as in the proof of Theorem
6.8
.

(ii) The natural map
H^{r}(F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}))\to F^{r}H^{r}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})
is an isomorphism.

\bullet

Dagger Stein varieties and dagger affinoids.

Corollary 6.19
.

Let
X
be a smooth dagger Stein variety or a smooth dagger affinoid over
C
.
Let
r\geq 0
. We have
the bicartesian diagram in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf Q}_{p}})
(recall that all cohomologies are classical)

(6.20)

\textstyle{H^{r}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{\operatorname{st}}(r)}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{\operatorname{dR}}(r)}
\textstyle{(H^{r}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\bf B}_{{\rm st}}^{+})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}\otimes\iota}
\textstyle{H^{r}F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{r}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})}

Proof.

Consider the following diagram of maps of distinguished triangles in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf Q}_{p}})

\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{1}}
\scriptstyle{{\beta}}
\textstyle{[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}]^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}\otimes\iota}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}\otimes\iota}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F^{r}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{2}}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r}}

Since
H^{i}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r})=0
for
i\geq r
, the maps
f_{1},f_{2}
are surjective in degrees
\geq r
. It suffices to show that
\operatorname{Ker}f_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\operatorname{Ker}f_{2}
in degree
r
.
We have the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Ker}f_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{r}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{1}}
\textstyle{(H^{r}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\bf B}_{{\rm st}}^{+})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}\otimes\iota}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Ker}f_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\vartheta}
\textstyle{H^{r}F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\vartheta}
\textstyle{H^{r}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\vartheta}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Ker}f_{2,C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{r}F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{2,C}}
\textstyle{H^{r}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)}

We claim that the map
\operatorname{Ker}f_{2}\to\operatorname{Ker}f_{2,C}
is an isomorphism.
Indeed, we compute

\displaystyle\operatorname{Ker}f_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\leftarrow}}\operatorname{Coker}(H^{r-1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{K})\widehat{\otimes}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\to H^{r-1}(({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{K})\widehat{\otimes}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}(\Omega^{r-1}(X_{K})/\operatorname{Ker}d)\widehat{\otimes}_{K}C,

\displaystyle\operatorname{Ker}f_{2,C}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\leftarrow}}(\Omega^{r-1}(X_{K})/\operatorname{Ker}d)\widehat{\otimes}_{K}C.

∎

6.4.2.
Conjecture

We will formulate now a conjecture
describing pro-étale cohomology in terms of the de Rham complex.

Conjecture 6.21
.

(
C_{\operatorname{st}}
-conjecture)

Let
X
be a smooth dagger variety over
C
.
Let
r\geq i
. The commutative diagram

\textstyle{\widetilde{H}^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\widetilde{H}^{i}_{\rm HK}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widetilde{H}^{i}F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widetilde{H}^{i}_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})}

is bicartesian. That is, the following sequence

(6.22)

0\to\widetilde{H}^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to(\widetilde{H}^{i}_{\rm HK}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}\oplus\widetilde{H}^{i}F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\to\widetilde{H}^{i}_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\to 0

is exact in
{\rm LH}(C_{{\bf Q}_{p}})
.

Remark 6.23
.

(i) This conjecture is known so far in the following cases:

•

X
is proper (see Examples
6.4.1
). In this case, the two horizontal arrows are injective.

•

X
is Stein or affinoid (see Examples
6.4.1
). In this case, the two horizontal arrows are surjective
and their kernels are
\Omega^{r-1}(X)/\operatorname{Ker}\,d
.

(ii) Let
X
be a smooth dagger variety over
C
. If
r\geq i
, set

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}^{r,i}:=\widetilde{H}^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))

\displaystyle\widetilde{X}^{r,i}:=(\widetilde{H}^{i}_{\rm HK}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}^{r,i}:=\widetilde{H}^{i}(F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}))

\displaystyle\widetilde{B}^{i}:=\widetilde{H}^{i}_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})

and denote by
H^{r,i}
,
X^{r,i}
, etc. the images of
\widetilde{H}^{r,i}
,
\widetilde{X}^{r,i}
, etc. in

C_{{\bf Q}_{p}}
.
Note that the
\widetilde{X}^{r,i}
’s and
\widetilde{B}^{i}
’s are classical, i.e.,
\widetilde{X}^{r,i}\simeq{X}^{r,i}

and
\widetilde{B}^{i}\simeq B^{i}
.

We have a commutative diagram with exact rows and columns:

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widetilde{H}^{r,i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{\widetilde{F}^{r,i}\oplus\widetilde{X}^{r,i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{t}
\textstyle{\widetilde{B}^{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t}
\textstyle{\widetilde{H}^{r+1,i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widetilde{F}^{r+1,i}\oplus\widetilde{X}^{r+1,i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widetilde{B}^{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\phantom{XXX}H^{i}_{\rm HK}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}}
\textstyle{H^{i}_{\rm dR}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\phantom{XXX}0}
\textstyle{0}

The vertical maps are multiplications by
t
(on pro-étale cohomology, this corresponds
to the Tate twist); for the isomorphism
X^{r+1,i}/tX^{r,i}\simeq H^{i}_{\rm HK}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}C
,
see Remark
5.16
; the isomorphism
\widetilde{F}^{r+1,i}\simeq t\widetilde{F}^{r,i}
comes from
the fact that
\tau_{\leq i}F^{r+1}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})=tF^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})
since
i\leq r
;
the bottom isomorphism is the Hyodo-Kato map. It follows
that, for fixed
i
, the conjecture for
r
is equivalent to the conjecture for
r+1
.
Hence it is enough to prove it for one
r\geq i
(for example
r=i
).

(iii) Since the
\widetilde{B}^{i}
’s are actually classical, and we have a long exact sequence

\cdots\to\widetilde{X}^{r,i-1}\oplus\widetilde{F}^{r,i-1}\to\widetilde{B}^{i-1}\to\widetilde{H}^{r,i}\to\widetilde{X}^{r,i}\oplus\widetilde{F}^{r,i}\to\widetilde{B}^{i}\to\cdots

it is enough, thanks to an induction on
i
, to prove surjectivity of

{X}^{r,i}\oplus{F}^{r,i}\to{B}^{i}
: this will show
that
\widetilde{X}^{r,i}\oplus\widetilde{F}^{r,i}\to\widetilde{B}^{i}
is surjective (since
\widetilde{B}^{i}\simeq B^{i}
)
and that the long exact sequence splits into short exact sequences, as wanted.

(iv) We will prove this conjecture for many small varieties in Theorem
8.1
below.

7.
Equivalent conjectures

Let
X
be a smooth dagger variety over
C
. In this Chapter, we will first state and discuss four conjectures, a priori unrelated, on the cohomology of
X
:

\bullet

Conjecture
6.21
(already stated above) describes the
p
-adic pro-étale cohomology of
X
in terms of the de Rham complex.

The remaining conjectures assume
X
to be quasi-compact:

\bullet

Conjecture
7.1
gives a restriction on the Hodge filtration on the de Rham cohomology in terms of the slopes of Frobenius on the Hyodo-Kato cohomology.

\bullet

Conjecture
7.4
says that, even if huge, the pro-étale cohomology groups
{\mathbb{H}}^{r}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p})
have nevertheless
{\bf Q}_{p}
-dimension equal to

\dim_{C}H^{r}_{\rm dR}(X)
.

\bullet

Conjecture
7.3
says that
{\mathbb{H}}^{r}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p})
is of curvature
\leq 0
.

Next, we proceed to studying these conjectures in the case
X
is quasi-compact. We use:

—

the period quasi-isomorphism between pro-étale cohomology and syntomic cohomology
from
[
18
, 6.10]
,

—

the canonical distinguished triangle involving syntomic cohomology from
[
18
, 5.12]
,

—

delicate properties of BC’s (scattered through
[
10
,
11
,
37
,
40
]

and recalled in Section
3.1
for the convenience of the reader).

These ingredients allow us to prove that the four conjectures above are, in fact,
equivalent (see Proposition
7.10
as well as Corollary
7.11

for a precise statement).

7.1.
Conjectures

7.1.1.
Acyclicity of de Rham cohomology

For a smooth dagger variety
X
over
C
, we set

(1)

F^{i}H^{r}_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}):=\operatorname{Im}(H^{r}(F^{i}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}))\to H^{r}_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}))
;

(2)

H^{r}_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}):=H^{r}_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\widehat{\otimes}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}}{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}
and equip it with the induced filtration.

Conjecture 7.1
.

Let
X
be a smooth quasi-compact dagger variety over
C
. For all
r
, the map

(H^{r}_{\rm HK}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}\to H^{r}_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r}

is surjective, i.e.
19
19

19

See Remark
5.16
.
,
the filtered
(\varphi,N)
-module
(H^{r}_{\rm HK}(X),F^{0}H^{r}_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}))
is acyclic.

Remark 7.2
.

If
X
is proper,
the pair
(H^{r}_{\rm HK}(X),F^{0}H^{r}_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}))
is weakly admissible, hence

X
verifies the conjecture.
If
X
is affinoid, then
H^{r}_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r}=0

and
X
clearly satisfies the conjecture.

7.1.2.
Curvature and height of pro-étale cohomology

Let
X
be a smooth quasi-compact dagger variety over
C
.
We show below (Corollary
7.9
) that
{\mathbb{H}}^{r}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p})
is naturally a qBC.

Conjecture 7.3
.

Then, for all
r\geq 0
,

{\mathbb{H}}^{r}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p})
has curvature
\leq 0
.

Conjecture 7.4
.

For all
r
,

{\rm ht}({\mathbb{H}}^{r}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}))=\dim_{C}H^{r}_{\rm dR}(X).

Remark 7.5
.

(i) If
X
is proper, Conjecture
7.4
is a theorem:
we have the exact sequence of BC’s (see Section
6.2.3
)

0\to H^{r}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to(H^{r}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\otimes_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\bf B}_{{\rm st}}^{+})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}\to H^{r}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r})\to 0

We know that

H^{r}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p})
has finite dimension over
{\bf Q}_{p}
; hence its height is
equal to its dimension over

{\bf Q}_{p}
. We also know that the slopes of Frobenius on
H^{r}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)
are
\leq r
,
which implies that
{\rm ht}((H^{r}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\otimes_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\bf B}_{{\rm st}}^{+})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}})=\dim_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}H^{r}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)
. Now,
the above short exact sequence implies that

{\rm ht}({\mathbb{H}}^{r}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}))=\dim_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}H^{r}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)
and,
since by the Hyodo-Kato isomorphism
\dim_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}H^{r}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)=\dim_{C}H^{r}_{\rm dR}(X)
,
we have

\dim_{{\bf Q}_{p}}(H^{r}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}))=\dim_{C}H^{r}_{\rm dR}(X),

as wanted.

(ii) Does there exist non proper dagger varieties
X
such that

H^{r}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p})
is finite dimensional over
{\bf Q}_{p}
for all
r
?
Already for
r=1
and
X
connected, one needs
{\mathscr{O}}(X)=C
.

7.2.
Equivalence of conjectures

In this section we assume
X
to be a smooth dagger variety over
C
such that

H^{i}_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})
is (free) of finite rank over
{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}
for all
i

(for example,
X
could be quasi-compact,
or a naive interior
20
20

20

See
[
18
, proof of Cor. 3.6]
for a precise definition.
of a quasi-compact,
or the analytification of an algebraic variety). We call such varieties
small
.
Conjectures
6.21
,
7.1
,
7.3
, and
7.4
make sense in this, slightly more general, set-up.

7.2.1.
The key diagram

Fix
r
and, for
i\leq r
, set

\displaystyle H^{r,i}:=H^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r)),

\displaystyle X^{r,i}:=(H^{i}_{\rm HK}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}},

\displaystyle F^{r,i}:=H^{i}(F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})),

\displaystyle{\rm DR}^{r,i}:=H^{i}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r}),

\displaystyle B^{i}:=H^{i}_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})

\displaystyle{\rm Fil}^{r,i}:={\rm Im}(F^{r,i}\to B^{i})

We also denote by

{\mathbb{H}}^{r,i},\quad{\mathbb{X}}^{r,i},\quad{\mathbb{F}}^{r,i},\quad{\mathbb{DR}}^{r,i},\quad{\mathbb{B}}^{i},\quad{\mathbb{F}}\mathbbm{il}^{r,i}

the associated TVS’s.

The isomorphism between pro-étale and syntomic cohomologies (see
[
18
, 6.19]
) yields a commutative diagram with exact rows:

\textstyle{H^{r,i-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X^{r,i-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm DR}^{r,i-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{r,i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X^{r,i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm DR}^{r,i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F^{r,i-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B^{i-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm DR}^{r,i-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F^{r,i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B^{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm DR}^{r,i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F^{r,i-1}/t^{r}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B^{i-1}/t^{r}{\rm Fil}^{r,i-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm DR}^{r,i-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F^{r,i}/t^{r}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B^{i}/t^{r}{\rm Fil}^{r,i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm DR}^{r,i}}

The bottom sequence is exact because
t^{r}F^{r,i}\to t^{r}{\rm Fil}^{r,i}
is an isomorphism
(multiplying by
t^{r}
kills the big
{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}
-torsion in
F^{r,i}
, and
B^{i}

is a free finite rank
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-module thanks to the Hyodo-Kato isomorphism), hence we
have an isomorphism:

(7.6)

\operatorname{Ker}(F^{r,i}\to F^{r,i}/t^{r})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\operatorname{Ker}(B^{i}\to B^{i}/t^{r}{\rm Fil}^{r,i}).

All spaces in the diagram are
C
-points of TVS’s and those
in the top and the bottom rows are
C
-points of qBC’s (this is clear for all of them except for

H^{r,i}
(and
H^{r,i-1}
), for which this is proven in Corollary
7.9
),
and the above diagram lifts to a diagram of TVS’s
(see
[
18
, Sec. 7]
).

7.2.2.
The canonical filtration on
{\mathbb{H}}^{r,i}

If
n=0,1
, set:

{\mathbbm{h}}^{n}(H^{i}_{\rm HK}\{-r\}):={\mathbb{H}}^{n}(X_{\rm FF},{\mathscr{E}}(H^{i}_{\rm HK}(X)\{-r\},F^{r}H^{i}_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}))).

Lemma 7.7
.

For all
i\leq r
, we have the following isomorphisms:

\displaystyle{\mathbbm{h}}^{0}(H^{i}_{\rm HK}\{-r\})

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm Ker}\big({\mathbb{X}}^{r,i}\oplus{\mathbb{F}}\mathbbm{il}^{r,i}\to{\mathbb{B}}^{i}\big)\simeq{\rm Ker}\big({\mathbb{X}}^{r,i}\to{\mathbb{B}}^{i}/{\mathbb{F}}\mathbbm{il}^{r,i}\big)

\displaystyle{\mathbbm{h}}^{1}(H^{i}_{\rm HK}\{-r\})

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm Coker}\big({\mathbb{X}}^{r,i}\oplus{\mathbb{F}}\mathbbm{il}^{r,i}\to{\mathbb{B}}^{i}\big)\simeq{\rm Coker}\big({\mathbb{X}}^{r,i}\to{\mathbb{B}}^{i}/{\mathbb{F}}\mathbbm{il}^{r,i}\big)

\displaystyle{\rm Ker}\big({\mathbb{H}}^{r,i}\to{\mathbb{X}}^{r,i}\oplus{\mathbb{F}}^{r,i}\big)

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm Coker}\big({\mathbb{X}}^{r,i-1}\oplus{\mathbb{F}}^{r,i-1}\to{\mathbb{B}}^{i-1}\big)\simeq{\mathbbm{h}}^{1}(H^{i-1}_{\rm HK}\{-r\})

Proof.

The first two isomorphisms are clear.
Using the snake lemma in the following commutative diagram with exact rows we get the last isomorphism:

(7.8)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{X}}^{r,i-1}/{\mathbb{H}}^{r,i-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{DR}}^{r,i-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm Ker}\big({\mathbb{H}}^{r,i}\to{\mathbb{X}}^{r,i}\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{B}}^{i-1}/{\mathbb{F}}\mathbbm{il}^{r,i-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{DR}}^{r,i-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm Ker}\big({\mathbb{F}}^{r,i}\to{\mathbb{B}}^{i}\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

∎

Corollary 7.9
.

(i)

{\mathbb{H}}^{r,i}
is a qBC. Moreover:

\bullet

({\mathbb{H}}^{r,i})_{>0}\simeq{\mathbbm{h}}^{1}(H^{i-1}_{\rm HK}\{-r\})
,

\bullet

({\mathbb{H}}^{r,i})_{<0}\simeq{\mathbbm{h}}^{0}(H^{i}_{\rm HK}\{-r\})
.

\bullet

({\mathbb{H}}^{r,i})_{=0}\simeq{\rm Ker}\big({\mathbb{F}}^{r,i}\to{\mathbb{B}}^{i}\big)=({\mathbb{F}}^{r,i})_{{\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}{\text{-tors}}}
.

(ii)
We have an exact sequence

0\to{\mathbbm{h}}^{1}(H^{i-1}_{\rm HK}\{-r\})\to{\mathbb{H}}^{r,i}\to{\mathbb{F}}^{r,i}\oplus{\mathbb{X}}^{r,i}\to{\mathbb{B}}^{i}\to{\mathbbm{h}}^{1}(H^{i}_{\rm HK}\{-r\})\to 0

Proof.

By diagram (
7.8
),

{\mathbb{H}}^{r,i}
has a natural filtration with successive quotients

{\rm Coker}\big({\mathbb{X}}^{r,i-1}\to{\mathbb{B}}^{i-1}/{\mathbb{F}}\mathbbm{il}^{r,i-1}\big)
,

{\mathbb{DR}}^{r,i-1}/({\mathbb{B}}^{i-1}/{\mathbbm{Fil}}^{r,i-1})

and
{\rm Ker}({\mathbb{X}}^{r,i}\to{\mathbb{DR}}^{r,i})
.

The first is equal to
{\mathbbm{h}}^{1}(H^{i-1}_{\rm HK}\{-r\})
, hence is
a BC of curvature
>0
being the
{\mathbb{H}}^{1}
of a vector bundle on
X_{\rm FF}

(one can also combine Proposition
3.23
and Lemma
5.17
);
the last
is a BC of curvature
<0
(as a sub-BC of a BC of curvature
<0
), equal to

{\mathbbm{h}}^{0}(H^{i}_{\rm HK}\{-r\})
(the map
{\mathbb{X}}^{r,i}\to{\mathbb{DR}}^{r,i}
factors
through
{\mathbb{X}}^{r,i}\to{\mathbb{B}}^{i}
);
the middle one is a
{\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}
-Pair isomorphic to
{\rm Ker}\big({\mathbb{F}}^{r,i}\to{\mathbb{B}}^{i}\big)

thanks to diagram (
7.8
). This proves (i).

(ii) follows from the last two points of Lemma
7.7
.
∎

7.2.3.
Equivalence of the conjectures

Proposition 7.10
.

The following properties are equivalent:

(a)
The diagram in Conjecture
6.21
is bicartesian.

(b)

{\mathbbm{h}}^{1}(H^{i}_{\rm HK}\{-r\})=0
, for
i=r-1
and
i=r
.

(c)

(H^{i}_{\rm HK}(X),F^{0}H^{i}_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}))

is acyclic, for
i=r-1
and
i=r
.

(d)

{\mathbb{H}}^{r,r}
and
21
21

21

Here
{\mathbb{H}}^{r,r+1}
stands for syntomic
cohomology since we are outside of the range of validity of the comparison with pro-étale
cohomology.

{\mathbb{H}}^{r,r+1}
have curvature
\leq 0
.

(e)

{\rm ht}({\mathbb{H}}^{r,r})=\dim_{C}H^{r}_{\rm dR}(X)
.

Proof.

The equivalence between (a) and (b) is a direct consequence of (ii) of Corollary
7.9
.
That between (b) and (c) is the invariance of acyclicity by “Tate twist”.

The equivalence between (b) and (d) is a direct consequence of Corollary
7.9
.

It remains to prove that (b) and (e) are equivalent. But, by definition

{\rm ht}({\mathbb{H}}^{r,r})={\rm ht}(({\mathbb{H}}^{r,r})^{>0})+{\rm ht}(({\mathbb{H}}^{r,r})^{<0})
,
and one gets from Corollary
7.9
and Remark
3.11
that

\displaystyle{\rm ht}({\mathbb{H}}^{r,r})

\displaystyle={\rm ht}({\mathbbm{h}}^{1}(H^{r-1}_{\rm HK}\{-r\}))+{\rm ht}({\mathbbm{h}}^{0}(H^{r}_{\rm HK}\{-r\}))

\displaystyle={\rm ht}({\mathbbm{h}}^{1}(H^{r-1}_{\rm HK}\{-r\}))+\dim H^{r}_{\rm HK}(X)+{\rm ht}({\mathbbm{h}}^{1}(H^{r}_{\rm HK}\{-r\}))

and the equivalence between (b) and (e) follows from the Hyodo-Kato isomorphism and the fact that

{\rm ht}({\mathbbm{h}}^{1}(H^{i}_{\rm HK}\{-r\}))<0
if
{\mathbbm{h}}^{1}(H^{i}_{\rm HK}\{-r\})\neq 0
.
∎

Corollary 7.11
.

If
X
is quasi-compact,
Conjectures
6.21
,
7.1
,
7.3
, and
7.4

are equivalent.

Proof.

Equivalence of (a) and (e) from Proposition
7.10
shows that,
already for a fixed
r
, Conjectures

6.21
and
7.4
are equivalent.
Equivalence between (a) and (c) show that they are equivalent to
Conjecture
7.1
(but one has to vary
r
). And equivalence between (c) and (d),
together with the twist invariance of (ii) of Remark
6.23
(to get back to a range
where syntomic is isomorphic to proétale) show that they are equivalent
to Conjecture
7.3
.
∎

Remark 7.12
.

It is easy to see that Corollary
7.11
holds, more generally, if
H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+})
is
of finite rank over
{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}
for all
i
. The proof is the same as in the quasi-compact case.

7.3.
Decomposing syntomic cohomology

The purpose of this section is to show that the canonical filtration
on
{\mathbb{H}}^{r,i}(X)
(see Corollary
7.9
and its proof) splits (noncanonically) if
r\geq\dim X
.
This result will not be used later.
As remarked in Remark
4.8
, is it not impossible that such a splitting exists for
all qBC.

In what follows,
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm cris}(X)
is a functorial complex representing log-crystalline
cohomology of
X
(built from an étale hypercovering of
X
by semi-stable formal schemes),
and
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+})
is obtained from
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm cris}(X)
by completion. Below we take the
\breve{C}
-version of Hyodo-Kato cohomology.

Lemma 7.13
.

Let
X
be a quasi-compact dagger variety over
C
and let
r\geq\dim X
.

(i)

H^{i}([{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm cris}(X)]^{\varphi=p^{r}})\simeq(H^{i}_{\rm HK}(X)\otimes{\bf B}_{{\rm st}}^{+})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}
as BC’s.

(ii)

[{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm cris}(X)]^{\varphi=p^{r}}
is quasi-isomorphic to
the direct sum of its cohomology groups.

Proof.

Claim (i) is proved in
[
18
, Chap. 7]
.
Recall that we have a (not Galois equivariant) quasi-isomorphism

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm cris}(X)\simeq{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm HK}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}_{{\rm cris}}^{+}.

Fix
i
. Then
H^{i}_{\rm HK}(X)
is a finite dimensional
\breve{C}
-vector space
with a semi-linear and bijective
\varphi
. Using Dieudonné-Manin’s theorem,
we can write
H^{i}_{\rm HK}(X)\simeq\oplus_{j\in J_{i}}D_{j}
with
D_{j}
irreducible,
of slope
\frac{a_{j}}{h_{j}}\leq\inf(i,\dim X)
, with
(a_{j},h_{j})=1
: i.e.,
D_{j}=\breve{C}e_{j}\oplus\breve{C}\varphi(e_{j})\oplus\cdots\oplus\breve{C}\varphi^{h_{j}-1}(e_{j})
with

\varphi^{h_{j}}(e_{j})=p^{a_{j}}e_{j}
. Since
r\geq\dim X
, we have

(D_{j}\otimes_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}_{{\rm cris}}^{+})^{\varphi=p^{r}}=\{xe_{j}+p^{-r}\varphi(xe_{j})+\cdots+p^{-r(h_{j}-1)}\varphi^{h_{j}-1}(xe_{j})|\varphi^{h_{j}}(x)=p^{rh_{j}-a_{j}}x\}

i.e.,
(D_{j}\otimes_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}_{{\rm cris}}^{+})^{\varphi=p^{r}}\simeq{\mathbb{U}}_{h_{j},rh_{j}-a_{j}}e_{j}
.
Choose
\tilde{e}_{j}\in Z^{i}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm cris}(X))
whose image in
H^{i}_{\rm HK}(X)\otimes_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}_{{\rm cris}}^{+}
is
e_{j}
.
If
\lambda\in{\mathbb{U}}_{h_{j},rh_{j}-a_{j}}
, then

(1-\tfrac{\varphi}{p^{r}})\big(\lambda\tilde{e}_{j}+p^{-r}\varphi(\lambda\tilde{e}_{j})+\cdots+p^{-r(h_{j}-1)}\varphi^{h_{j}-1}(\lambda\tilde{e}_{j})\big)=\lambda(\tilde{e}_{j}-p^{-a_{j}}\varphi^{h_{j}}(\tilde{e}_{j})).

Since
\varphi^{h_{j}}(e_{j})=p^{a_{j}}e_{j}
, it follows that
\tilde{e}_{j}-p^{-a_{j}}\varphi^{h_{j}}(\tilde{e}_{j})=dx_{j}
.
Hence
(\lambda_{j})_{j}\mapsto(\lambda_{j}e_{j},\lambda_{j}x_{j})
maps

\oplus_{j\in J_{j}}{\mathbb{U}}_{h_{j},rh_{j}-a_{j}}
to a subcomplex of the total complex of the above mapping fiber,
with all arrows
0
and all cohomology groups
0
except
H^{i}
which is, by construction,
indentified with
H^{i}([{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm cris}(X)]^{\varphi=p^{r}})
.
The lemma follows.
∎

Remark 7.14
.

The arrow

\oplus_{i}(H^{i}_{\rm HK}(X)\otimes_{\breve{C}}{\bf B}_{{\rm st}}^{+})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}[-i]\to[\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 24.53546pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-24.53546pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm cris}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.49599pt\raise 6.5pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{1-{\varphi}/{p^{r}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 48.53546pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 48.53546pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm cris}(X)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces]

that we constructed above depends on the choice of the
\tilde{e}_{j}
.
This arrow can be used to pin down a subspace of
Z^{i}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}))
isomorphic
to
H^{i}_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+})
, namely the
{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}
-module generated by the images of

\lambda\tilde{e}_{j}+p^{-r}\varphi(\lambda\tilde{e}_{j})+\cdots+p^{-r(h_{j}-1)}\varphi^{h_{j}-1}(\lambda\tilde{e}_{j})
for
\lambda\in{\bf Q}_{p^{h_{i}}}

and
j\in J_{i}
.

Proposition 7.15
.

Let
r\geq\dim X
.
We have a decomposition

{\mathbb{H}}^{i}_{\rm syn}(X,r)\simeq{\mathbbm{h}}^{1}(H^{i-1}_{\rm HK}\{-r\})\oplus{\mathbbm{h}}^{0}(H^{i}_{\rm HK}\{-r\})\oplus(Y^{i-1},Z^{i})

where

(Y^{i-1},Z^{i})
is a (reasonable)
{\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}
-Pair.

Proof.

Let us write syntomic cohomology as the fiber

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm syn}(X,r)=\big[[{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm cris}(X)]^{\varphi=p^{r}}\oplus F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+})\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+})\big]

and replace
[{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm cris}(X)]^{\varphi=p^{r}}
by the direct sum of its cohomology groups,
the induced map to
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+})
being induced by the choice of the
\tilde{e}_{j}
as above
(the induced map on cohomology does not depend on this choice).
It follows that
H^{i}_{\rm syn}(X,r)
is
H^{1}
of the following complex
(where
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}^{s}
and
F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}^{s}
are the terms of degree
s
in the respective complexes
and
{\rm HK}^{s}:=(H^{s}_{\rm HK}(X)\otimes{\bf B}_{{\rm st}}^{+})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}
)

{\rm HK}^{i-1}\oplus F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}^{i-1}\oplus{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}^{i-2}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{d^{i-1}_{\rm tot}}}{\rm HK}^{i}\oplus(F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}^{i})^{d=0}\oplus{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}^{i-1}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{d^{i}_{\rm tot}}}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}^{i})^{d=0}.

Now,
H^{1}
does not change if we mod out by the complex
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}^{i-2}\to{\rm Im}\,d
to obtain the complex

(7.16)

{\rm HK}^{i-1}\oplus F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}^{i-1}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{d^{i-1}_{\rm tot}}}{\rm HK}^{i}\oplus(F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}^{i})^{d=0}\oplus{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}^{i-1}/({\rm Im}\,d)\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{d^{i}_{\rm tot}}}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}^{i})^{d=0}.

But

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}^{i-1}/({\rm Im}\,d)
is an extension of
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}^{i-1}/({\rm Ker}\,d)

by
H^{i-1}_{\rm dR}
(where
H^{s}_{\rm dR}:=H^{s}_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+})
). Such an extension
is split
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-linearly since
H^{i-1}_{\rm dR}
is free of finite rank over
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
and
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}^{i-1}/({\rm Ker}\,d)
is
separated: pick a set of continuous
{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}
-linear forms on
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}^{i-1}/({\rm Im}\,d)
that
form a basis of
{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}
-linear forms on
H^{i-1}_{\rm dR}
(these exist because all the
spaces involved are of compact type hence reflexive), then the common kernel
of these linear forms is a supplementary subspace of
H^{i-1}_{\rm dR}
that maps homeomorphically
to
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}^{i-1}/({\rm Ker}\,d)
by the open mapping theorem.
We can then mod out (
7.16
) by
0\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}^{i-1}/({\rm Ker}\,d)\to{\rm Im}\,d

and obtain that
H^{i}_{\rm syn}(X,r)
is
H^{1}
of the complex:

{\rm HK}^{i-1}\oplus F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}^{i-1}\to{\rm HK}^{i}\oplus(F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}^{i})^{d=0}\oplus H_{\rm dR}^{i-1}\to H^{i}_{\rm dR.}

We have splittings (
F^{r}H^{i}_{\rm dR}
and
F^{r}H^{i-1}_{\rm dR}
are free of finite rank over
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
,
so it is easy to lift them)

\displaystyle(F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}^{i})^{d=0}

\displaystyle\simeq F^{r}H^{i}_{\rm dR}\oplus\hat{Z}^{i},\quad{\text{where $\hat{Z}^{i}={\rm Ker}((F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}^{i})^{d=0}\to H^{i}_{\rm dR})$}};

\displaystyle F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}^{i-1}/{\rm Ker}\,d_{\rm tot}

\displaystyle\simeq F^{r}H^{i-1}_{\rm dR}\oplus\hat{Y}^{i-1},\quad{\text{with $\hat{Y}^{i-1}=F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}^{i-1}/{\rm Ker}\,d$}}.

It follows that

H^{i}_{\rm syn}(X,r)\simeq(\hat{Y}^{i-1},\hat{Z}^{i})\oplus{\rm Ker}({\rm HK}^{i}\oplus F^{r}H^{i}_{\rm dR}\to H^{i}_{\rm dR})\oplus{\rm Coker}\,({\rm HK}^{i-1}\oplus F^{r}H^{i-1}_{\rm dR}\to H^{i-1}_{\rm dR})

The Ker (resp. Coker) term gives us the
{\mathbbm{h}}^{0}
(resp.
{\mathbbm{h}}^{1}
) term
in the statement of the proposition.
Now the image of
\hat{Y}^{i-1}\to\hat{Z}^{i}
contains
t^{r}\hat{Z}^{i}
(and even
t^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}^{i}
);
hence we can replace
(\hat{Y}^{i-1},\hat{Z}^{i})
by
the
{\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}
-Pair
(Y^{i-1},Z^{i})
with
Y^{i-1}=\hat{Y}^{i-1}/t^{r}
and

Z^{i}=\hat{Z}^{i}/t^{r}
.
∎

8.
De Rham-to-pro-étale comparison theorems

Using Corollary
7.11
we will now prove Conjecture
6.21
for some quasi-compact smooth dagger varieties and then, via a limit argument, for a product of a smooth proper and a smooth Stein variety.

8.1.
Small varieties

We will say that a smooth dagger variety

has de Rham slopes
\geq 0
if it is small and satisfies Conjecture
7.1

for all
r\geq 0
. We are going to prove that various dagger varieties have de Rham slopes
\geq 0
.
By Corollary
7.11
this implies that they satisfy Conjectures
6.21
,
7.3
, and
7.4
.

Theorem 8.1
.

Let
X
be a smooth dagger variety over
C
, which has one of the
following (non mutually exclusive) forms:

(a)
a product of two varieties with de Rham slopes
\geq 0
,

(b)
the analytification of an algebraic variety,

(c)
an almost proper smooth variety (i.e., the complement in a proper
smooth rigid analytic variety of a closed subvariety).

Then
X
has de Rham slopes
\geq 0
.

Proof.

We start with claim (a). For a quasi-compact smooth dagger variety
T
over
C
, let
M^{i}(T):=(H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(T),F^{0}H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(T/{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}))

be the associated filtered
(\varphi,N)
-modules. Let
X,Y
be small smooth dagger varieties over
C
that have de Rham slopes
\geq 0
.
We claim that
X\times Y
has de Rham slopes
\geq 0
as well.

Indeed, the Hyodo-Kato and de Rham cup products induce a pairing

M^{i}(X)\otimes M^{j}(Y)\to M^{i+j}(X\times Y),\quad i,j\in{\bf N}.

By functoriality of the functor
{\mathscr{E}}(-)
it gives a pairing

\gamma:{\mathscr{E}}(M^{i}(X))\otimes_{{\mathscr{O}}}{\mathscr{E}}(M^{j}(Y))\to{\mathscr{E}}(M^{i}(X)\otimes M^{j}(Y))\to{\mathscr{E}}(M^{i+j}(X\times Y)).

Since we have a Künneth formula for Hyodo-Kato cohomology (reduce, via the Hyodo-Kato quasi-isomorphism, to Künneth formula for de Rham cohomology), the map
\gamma
yields an exact sequence of coherent sheaves on
X_{\operatorname{FF}}

0\to\oplus_{i+j=n}{\mathscr{E}}(M^{i}(X))\otimes_{{\mathscr{O}}}{\mathscr{E}}(M^{j}(Y))\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\gamma}}{\mathscr{E}}(M^{n}(X\times Y))\to{\mathscr{F}}\to 0,

where
{\mathscr{F}}
is supported at
\infty
. Applying cohomology we get the exact sequence

\oplus_{i+j=n}H^{1}(X_{\operatorname{FF}},{\mathscr{E}}(M^{i}(X))\otimes_{{\mathscr{O}}}{\mathscr{E}}(M^{j}(Y)))\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\gamma}}H^{1}(X_{\operatorname{FF}},{\mathscr{E}}(M^{n}(X\times Y)))\to H^{1}(X_{\operatorname{FF}},{\mathscr{F}})\to 0

Since
H^{1}(X_{\operatorname{FF}},{\mathscr{F}})=0
and the tensor product of two acyclic vector bundles is acyclic (see Remark
5.15
), this yields
H^{1}(X_{\operatorname{FF}},{\mathscr{E}}(M^{n}(X\times Y)))=0
, as wanted.

We pass now to claim (b). Let
X
be a smooth algebraic variety over
C
and denote by
X^{\rm an}
its analytification.
We claim that
X^{\rm an}
has de Rham slopes
\geq 0
.
Indeed, the filtered
(\varphi,N)
-module
(H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X),F^{0}H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}))
,
i\in{\bf N}
, is admissible by
[
3
]
, hence acyclic. The natural map

\gamma:M^{i}(X):=(H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X),F^{0}H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}))\to(H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X^{\rm an}),F^{0}H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X^{\rm an}/{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}))=M^{i}(X^{\rm an})

is an isomorphism
on the first factor by Hyodo-Kato GAGA (see
[
47
]
). Applying the functor
{\mathscr{E}}(-)
, we get an exact sequence of coherent sheaves on
X_{\operatorname{FF}}

0\to{\mathscr{E}}(M^{i}(X))\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\gamma}}{\mathscr{E}}(M^{i}(X^{\rm an}))\to{\mathscr{F}}\to 0,

where
{\mathscr{F}}
is a coherent sheaf supported at
\infty
. Taking cohomology we get a short exact sequence

H^{1}(X_{\operatorname{FF}},{\mathscr{E}}(M^{i}(X)))\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\gamma}}H^{1}(X_{\operatorname{FF}},{\mathscr{E}}(M^{i}(X^{\rm an})))\to 0

Since
H^{1}(X_{\operatorname{FF}},{\mathscr{E}}(M^{i}(X)))=0
this yields
H^{1}(X_{\operatorname{FF}},{\mathscr{E}}(M^{i}(X^{\rm an})))=0
, as wanted.

Concerning claim (c),
let
X
be a proper smooth rigid analytic variety over
C
and let
Z\subset X
be a closed subvariety. Let
U=X\setminus Z
be the complement. We want to show that
U
is has de Rham slopes
\geq 0
. By resolution of singularities (see
[
8
]
) there exists a proper morphism
\pi:X^{\prime}\to X
such that
\pi^{-1}(Z)_{\rm red}
with its canonical reduced closed subspace structure is a simple normal crossing divisor. We have
U=X^{\prime}\setminus D
, hence, we may assume that
Z=D

is a divisor with simple normal crossings in
X
.

Let
i\leq r
. Consider the following map of exact sequences in
{\rm LH}(C_{{\bf Q}_{p}})

\textstyle{H^{i-1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X^{\times}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{H^{i}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(U,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

(H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(U)\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}

\oplus

H^{i}(F^{r}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma(X^{\times}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}))

\textstyle{H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X^{\times}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{H^{i-1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(U/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widetilde{H}^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(U,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{i,1}}

(H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(U)\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}

\oplus

\widetilde{H}^{i}(F^{r}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma(U/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}))

\scriptstyle{f_{i,2}}
\textstyle{H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(U/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})}

The top sequence is exact by
[
47
]
(see
[
16
, Cor. 1.10]
for the case of semistable reduction); here
H^{i-1}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X^{\times}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})
denotes
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology of the log-space
X^{\times}
with its logarithmic filtration. We note that
the group
H^{i}(F^{r}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma(X^{\times}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}))
is a finite length
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-module.
The diagram shows that the map
f_{i,2}
in the bottom sequence is surjective.
Since this works for all
i\leq r\in{\bf N}
, it implies that that the map
f_{i,1}
is injective, as wanted.
∎

Remark 8.2
.

The above proof shows that, in the case of almost proper
U
over
C
, the difference between the étale and pro-étale cohomology is the same as the difference between the Hodge filtration and the naive Hodge filtration:

[H^{i}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(U,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to\widetilde{H}^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(U,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))]\simeq[H^{i}(F^{r}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma(X^{\times}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}))\to\widetilde{H}^{i}(F^{r}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma(U/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}))],\quad i\leq r.

8.1.1.
Complements of tubular neighbourhoods

We will give two basic examples of complements of tubular neighbourhoods in proper smooth dagger
varieties that have de Rham slopes
\geq 0
. It should be possible to vastly generalize these examples,
but this would require other techniques than the ones used in the paper.

For the first example, we assume everything has semistable models.
Recall the following result of Grosse-Klönne
[
34
, Cor. 3.8]

Proposition 8.3
.

(1)

Let
\varpi\in{\mathscr{O}}_{K}
be a uniformizer. Let
\mathscr{Z}\hookrightarrow{\mathscr{X}}
be a closed immersion
of weak formal
{\mathscr{O}}_{K}
-schemes. Assume that locally on
{\mathscr{X}}
there exist étale morphisms of
weak formal
{\mathscr{O}}_{K}
-schemes

q:{\mathscr{X}}\to{\rm Spf}({\mathscr{O}}_{K}\{X_{1},...,X_{n}\}/(X_{1}...X_{r}-\varpi)),

such that
\mathscr{Z}=\cap_{j=r+1}^{m}V(q^{*}X_{j})
, for some
1\leq r\leq m\leq n
. Then the natural map

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}({\mathscr{X}}_{K}\setminus\mathscr{Z}_{K})\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(]{\mathscr{X}}_{k}\setminus\mathscr{Z}_{k}[_{{\mathscr{X}}})

is a filtered quasi-isomorphism.

(2)

In the setting of (1), we can replace
\mathscr{Z}=\cap_{j=r+1}^{m}V(q^{*}X_{j})
with
\mathscr{Z}=\cup_{j=r+1}^{m}V(q^{*}X_{j})
.

In the setting of Proposition
8.3
, assume that
{\mathscr{X}}_{K}
is proper and set
U:=]{\mathscr{X}}_{k}\setminus\mathscr{Z}_{k}[_{{\mathscr{X}}}
. We note that
U
is quasi-compact. The space
U_{C}
has de Rham slopes
\geq 0
because, via the Hyodo-Kato isomorphism and Proposition
8.3
, the canonical map

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}({\mathscr{X}}_{K}\setminus\mathscr{Z}_{K})\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(]{\mathscr{X}}_{k}\setminus\mathscr{Z}_{k}[_{{\mathscr{X}}})

is a quasi-isomorphism, the analogous map for
{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology is a filtered quasi-isomorphism,
and we can use claim (c) of Theorem
8.1
to deduce that the left-hand side has de Rham slopes
\geq 0

from which it follows that the right-hand side
has de Rham slopes
\geq 0
.

For the second example,
we don’t assume the existence of semistable models, but we remove a sufficiently small
tubular neighbourhood
of a smooth subvariety.
We have the following setting:

Proposition 8.4
.

Let
X
be a projective space over
K
and let
Z
be a smooth scheme-theoretic intersection
Z_{1}\times_{X}\cdots\times_{X}Z_{c}
of
c
hypersurfaces. Then, there exists an element
\varepsilon_{0}\in|K^{*}|
such that, for any
\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{0}
, the canonical morphisms

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X\setminus Z)\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X\setminus Z(\varepsilon)),\quad{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{C}\setminus Z_{C})\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{C}\setminus Z(\varepsilon)_{C})

are quasi-isomorphism (compatible with all the structures). Here
Z(\varepsilon)
is the tubular neighbourhood defined in
[
42
, proof of Prop. 8.7]
,
[
9
, Sec. 4]
.

Similarly for
Z
replaced by the union of
Z_{i}
’s.

Proof.

The Hyodo-Kato case follows from the de Rham case via the Hyodo-Kato isomorphism. For the de Rham case, note that, locally, we have an isomorphism

Y(\varepsilon)\simeq Y\times_{K}{\mathbb{B}}(\varepsilon)^{c},

where
{\mathbb{B}}(\varepsilon)
denotes the open disc of radius
\varepsilon
.
Hence the quasi-isomorphsm in our proposition follows from
[
34
, Prop. 3.7]
.

For the union of
Z_{i}
’s, we use the above case and a Čech analytic descent.
∎

The dagger variety
U(\varepsilon)_{C}:=X_{C}\setminus Z(\varepsilon)_{C}
has de Rham slopes
\geq 0

via Proposition
8.4
and claim (c) of Theorem
8.1
.

8.2.
Proper times Stein

In this section we prove Conjecture
6.21
for products of proper smooth and Stein varieties.

8.2.1.
Limit arguments

This is a preliminary computation for treating the case of partially proper varieties.
The general set-up is the following:
if
X
is a smooth dagger variety over
C
, we choose a covering
\{X_{n}\}_{n\in{\bf N}}
by
increasing
quasi-compact dagger subvarieties, and we want to deduce the result for
X
from the result
for the
X_{n}
’s. To make formulas less cumbersome, let us introduce some notation.

Set

(8.5)

\displaystyle X^{r,i}:=(H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{R}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}},

\displaystyle X^{r,i}_{n}:=(H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{n})\widehat{\otimes}^{R}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}},

\displaystyle F^{r,i}:=H^{i}(F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+})),

\displaystyle F^{r,i}_{n}:=H^{i}(F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{n}/{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+})),

\displaystyle A^{r,i}:={\rm Ker}(F^{r,i}\to H^{i}_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+})),

\displaystyle A^{r,i}_{n}:={\rm Ker}(F^{r,i}_{n}\to H^{i}_{\rm dR}(X_{n}/{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+})),

\displaystyle\overline{F}^{r,i}:=F^{r,i}/A^{r,i},

\displaystyle\overline{F}_{n}^{r,i}:=F_{n}^{r,i}/A_{n}^{r,i}.

So that, by definition,

(8.6)

\overline{F}^{r,i}\simeq F^{r}H^{i}_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}),\quad\overline{F}_{n}^{r,i}\simeq F^{r}H^{i}_{\rm dR}(X_{n}/{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}).

In particular,

\overline{F}^{0,i}\simeq H^{i}_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}),\quad\overline{F}_{n}^{0,i}\simeq H^{i}_{\rm dR}(X_{n}/{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}).

We want to prove the exactness of:

(8.7)

0\to H^{r,i}\to X^{r,i}\oplus F^{r,i}_{n}\to\overline{F}^{0,i}\to 0

Assume that we have the exact sequences

(8.8)

0\to H^{r,i}_{n}\to X^{r,i}_{n}\oplus F^{r,i}_{n}\to\overline{F}^{0,i}_{n}\to 0

Hence, to prove the exactness of (
8.7
), we need to control various
{\mathrm{R}}^{1}\operatorname{lim}_{n}
.

Remark 8.9
.

(i)
By
[
18
, Prop. 5.8]
and
[
18
, Prop. 5.12]
, respectively, we have

{\mathrm{R}}^{1}\operatorname{lim}_{n}X^{r,i}_{n}=0,\quad{\mathrm{R}}^{1}\operatorname{lim}_{n}\overline{F}^{r,i}_{n}=0

(
[
18
, Prop. 5.12]
proves the result for
\overline{F}^{0,i}_{n}
; the general case
follows because

\overline{F}^{r,i}_{n}
contains
t^{r}\overline{F}^{0,i}_{n}
,
and
\overline{F}^{r,i}_{n}/t^{r}\overline{F}^{0,i}_{n}
is a BC).

(ii) We have two exact sequences and isomorphisms

\displaystyle 0\to{\mathrm{R}}^{1}\operatorname{lim}_{n}F^{r,i-1}_{n}\to F^{r,i}\to\operatorname{lim}_{n}F^{r,i}_{n}\to 0,

\displaystyle 0\to{\mathrm{R}}^{1}\operatorname{lim}_{n}H^{r,i-1}_{n}\to H^{r,i}\to\operatorname{lim}_{n}H^{r,i}_{n}\to 0,

\displaystyle X^{r,i}\simeq\operatorname{lim}_{n}X^{r,i}_{n},\quad\overline{F}^{0,i}\simeq\operatorname{lim}_{n}\overline{F}^{0,i}_{n}.

(iii)
Applying
\operatorname{lim}_{n}
to (
6.11
) we obtain the exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\to\operatorname{lim}_{n}H^{r,i}_{n}

\displaystyle\to\operatorname{lim}_{n}X^{r,i}_{n}\oplus\operatorname{lim}_{n}F^{r,i}_{n}\to\operatorname{lim}_{n}\overline{F}^{0,i}_{n}\to{\mathrm{R}}^{1}\operatorname{lim}_{n}H^{r,i}_{n}\to{\mathrm{R}}^{1}\operatorname{lim}_{n}F^{r,i}_{n}\to 0

Hence, if we can prove that
{\mathrm{R}}^{1}\operatorname{lim}_{n}H^{r,i}_{n}=0
, for all
i
,
we get that
H^{r,i}\simeq\operatorname{lim}_{n}H^{r,i}_{n}
, that (
8.7
) is exact and, as a bonus, that

{\mathrm{R}}^{1}\operatorname{lim}_{n}F^{r,i}_{n}=0

and
F^{r,i}\simeq\operatorname{lim}_{n}F^{r,i}_{n}
.

Proving directly the vanishing of the
{\mathrm{R}}^{1}\operatorname{lim}_{n}H^{r,i}_{n}
’s for all
i

does not look too promising since pro-étale cohomology is not that easy to deal with, but one
can try to prove statements about the filtered de Rham complexes that imply this vanishing.
Set

{\rm DR}^{r,i}:=H^{i}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r}),\quad{\rm DR}^{r,i}_{n}:=H^{i}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{n}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r}).

Lemma 8.10
.

We have the implications

({\mathrm{R}}^{1}\operatorname{lim}_{n}{\rm DR}^{r,i-1}_{n}=0)\Longrightarrow({\mathrm{R}}^{1}\operatorname{lim}_{n}A^{r,i}_{n}=0)\Longrightarrow({\mathrm{R}}^{1}\operatorname{lim}_{n}H^{r,i}_{n}=0).

Proof.

We have a long exact sequence

\cdots\overline{F}^{0,i-1}_{n}\to{\rm DR}^{r,i-1}_{n}\to F^{r,i}_{n}\to\overline{F}^{0,i}_{n}\to\cdots

It follows that
A^{r,i}_{n}
is a quotient of
{\rm DR}^{r,i-1}_{n}
. This proves the first
implication.

For the second implication, note that
A^{r,i}_{n}
is naturally a subspace of
H^{r,i}_{n}
;
denote by
\overline{H}^{r,i}_{n}
the quotient.
Since
t^{r}\overline{F}^{0,i}_{n}
is a subspace of
\overline{F}^{r,i}_{n}
,
we have an exact sequence

0\to\overline{H}^{r,i}_{n}\to X^{r,i}_{n}\oplus(\overline{F}^{r,i}_{n}/t^{r}\overline{F}^{0,i}_{n})\to(\overline{F}^{0,i}_{n}/t^{r}\overline{F}^{0,i}_{n})\to 0

in which all the terms, except
\overline{H}^{r,i}_{n}
are BC’s. This shows that

\overline{H}^{r,i}_{n}
is a BC as well
22
22

22

To be more precise,
by
[
18
, Sec. 7.3]
, the exact sequence (
8.8
) can be lifted to the category of Vector Spaces. Moreover, since
H^{i}_{\rm dR}(X_{n})
is finite
dimensional, all the terms in this exact sequence are BC’s:
this is clear for
\overline{\mathbb{F}}^{0,i}/t^{r}\overline{\mathbb{F}}^{0,i}
and for

{\mathbb{X}}^{r,i}_{n}
; it is true for
\overline{\mathbb{F}}^{r,i}/t^{r}\overline{\mathbb{F}}^{0,i}

because it is
is a sub-
{\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}
-module of
\overline{\mathbb{F}}^{0,i}/t^{r}\overline{\mathbb{F}}^{0,i}
,
and, finally, it is true
for
\overline{\mathbb{H}}^{r,i}
as the kernel of a morphism between BC’s.
,
hence
{\mathrm{R}}^{1}\operatorname{lim}_{n}\overline{H}^{r,i}_{n}=0

by Remark
3.15
.
It follows that we have an exact sequence

0\to\lim_{n}A^{r,i}_{n}\to\lim_{n}H^{r,i}_{n}\to\lim_{n}\overline{H}^{r,i}_{n}\to{\mathrm{R}}^{1}\lim_{n}A^{r,i}_{n}\to{\mathrm{R}}^{1}\lim_{n}H^{r,i}_{n}\to 0

which proves the second implication.
∎

Remark 8.11
.

The above proof yields that:

{\mathrm{R}}^{1}\operatorname{lim}_{n}\overline{H}^{r,i}_{n}=0

8.2.2.
Proper times Stein

Let
X
be the dagger variety
X:=Y\times_{C}S
, where
Y
and
S
are smooth dagger varieties over
C
,
Y
is proper and
S
is Stein. We cover
S
with a strictly increasing admissible covering
\{S_{n}\}_{i\in{\bf N}}
by open dagger affinoids. Set
X_{n}:=Y\times_{C}S_{n}
.

Proposition 8.12
.

For
i\geq 0
, we have

{\mathrm{R}}^{1}\lim_{n}H^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{n},{\bf Q}_{p})=0.

Proof.

Since
{\mathrm{R}}\lim_{n}H^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{n},{\bf Q}_{p})={\mathrm{R}}\lim_{n}H^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(\widehat{X}_{n},{\bf Q}_{p})
, we can replace
X_{n}
,
S_{n}
with their completions.
Since
\widehat{S}_{n}
’s are quasi-compact, we can pass to étale cohomology.
Using Lemma
8.13
below and Theorem
6.14
we get

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}^{1}\lim_{n}H^{i}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(X_{n},{\bf Q}_{p})

\displaystyle\simeq\bigoplus_{j\geq 0}H^{j}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(Y,{\bf Q}_{p})\otimes_{{\bf Q}_{p}}{\mathrm{R}}^{1}\lim_{n}H^{i-j}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(\widehat{S}_{n},{\bf Q}_{p})=0,

as wanted.
∎

Lemma 8.13
.

Let
Y,T
be smooth rigid analytic varieties over
C
. Assume that
Y
is proper. Let
i\geq 0
.
We have the Künneth isomorphism

(8.14)

\bigoplus_{j\geq 0}H^{j}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(Y,{\bf Q}_{p})\otimes_{{\bf Q}_{p}}H^{i-j}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(T,{\bf Q}_{p})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}H^{i}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(Y\times_{C}T,{\bf Q}_{p}).

Proof.

\bullet

Step1: the integral Künneth formula.
We claim that the canonical map

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(Y,{\bf Z}_{p})\otimes_{{\bf Z}_{p}}^{L}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(T,{\bf Z}_{p})\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(Y\times_{C}T,{\bf Z}_{p})

is a quasi-isomorphism. The proof of Künneth formula from
[
46
, Tag 0F13]
goes through once we have the quasi-isomorphism

(8.15)

{\mathrm{R}}p_{2,*}{\bf Z}_{p}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\leftarrow}}\underline{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(Y,{\bf Z}_{p})},

where
p_{2}:Y\times_{C}T\to T
is the canonical projection. This is a consequence of the proper base change theorem of Bhatt-Hansen
[
6
, Th. 1.6]
. To recall the proof from
[
46
, Tag 0F13]
: we apply
{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(T,-)
to
the quasi-isomorphism (
8.15
) and get a quasi-isomorphism

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(Y\times_{C}T,{\bf Z}_{p})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\leftarrow}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(T,\underline{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(Y,{\bf Z}_{p})}).

Now, we use the fact that the complex
{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(T,\underline{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(Y,{\bf Z}_{p})})
is perfect and get a quasi-isomorphism

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(T,\underline{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(Y,{\bf Z}_{p})})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\leftarrow}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(T,{\bf Z}_{p})\otimes_{{\bf Z}_{p}}^{L}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(Y,{\bf Z}_{p}).

This finishes the proof of the integral Künneth formula.

\bullet

Step 2: passing to
{\bf Q}_{p}
-coefficients.
From Step 1 and the fact that the complex
{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(Y,{\bf Q}_{p})
is perfect, we get the canonical quasi-isomorphism

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(Y,{\bf Q}_{p})\otimes^{L}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(T,{\bf Q}_{p})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(Y\times_{C}T,{\bf Q}_{p}).

Taking cohomology of both sides we get the isomorphism in the proposition.
∎

Remark 8.16
.

(i) Proposition
8.12
implies the isomorphism

H^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\lim_{n}H^{i}_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(X_{n},{\bf Q}_{p}).

(ii)
The proof of Proposition
8.12
yields also the Künneth isomorphism

\bigoplus_{j\geq 0}H^{j}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(Y,{\bf Q}_{p})\otimes_{{\bf Q}_{p}}H^{i-j}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(S,{\bf Q}_{p})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}H^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}).

This Künneth decomposition is true because we are in the very special case where the cohomology
groups of one of the factors are finite dimensional.
Without this assumption, such a decomposition already fails in the simplest case where both
X
and
Y

are the open unit ball in dimension
1
.

Proposition 8.17
.

Conjecture
6.21
holds for
X=Y\times_{C}S
as above.

Proof.

Let
i\leq r
. By Remark
6.23
, we reduce to showing that the sequence of algebraic cohomologies

0\to{H}^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to({H}^{i}_{\rm HK}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}\oplus{H}^{i}F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\to{H}^{i}_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\to 0

is exact. Granting Proposition
8.12
, this follows from (iii) of Remark
8.9
and Section
8.1
.
∎

9.
Pro-étale–to–de Rham comparison theorems

In this chapter we propose a recipe to extract,
from the pro-étale cohomology of varieties defined over
C
,
the Hyodo-Kato and de Rham cohomologies (as modules over the relevant rings)
and, for varieties
defined over
K
, to extract also Frobenius, monodromy, and the naive Hodge filtration.

9.1.
The pro-étale–to–de Rham
C_{\rm st}
-conjecture for varieties over
K

In this section, we study the following conjecture extracting, for analytic spaces over
K
,
the Hyodo-Kato and de Rham cohomologies
from the pro-étale cohomology. This extends to
p
-adic analytic spaces
the
C_{\rm st}
-conjecture of Fontaine.

Conjecture 9.1
.

Let
X
be a smooth dagger variety over
K
. We have natural strict isomorphisms:

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}^{\rm sm}_{{\mathscr{G}}_{K}}(H^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}),{\bf B}_{{\rm st}})\simeq H^{i}_{{\rm HK}}(X_{C})^{\boldsymbol{*}},\quad{\text{as a $(\varphi,N,{\mathscr{G}}_{K})$-module,}}

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}_{{\mathscr{G}}_{K}}(H^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}),{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}})\simeq H^{i}_{{\rm dR}}(X)^{\boldsymbol{*}},\quad{\text{as a filtered $K$-module}}.

Remark 9.2
.

Our approach uses syntomic cohomology, which gives a description of

H^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))
for
r\geq i
, rather than that of
H^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p})
.
Hence we are going to consider the following equivalent form of Conjecture
9.1

(where the
\{r\}
has the same meaning as in Corollary
5.10
):

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}^{\rm sm}_{{\mathscr{G}}_{K}}(H^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)),{\bf B}_{{\rm st}})\simeq H^{i}_{{\rm HK}}(X_{C})^{\boldsymbol{*}}\{r\},\quad{\text{as a $(\varphi,N,{\mathscr{G}}_{K})$-module,}}

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}_{{\mathscr{G}}_{K}}(H^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)),{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}})\simeq H^{i}_{{\rm dR}}(X)^{\boldsymbol{*}}\{r\},\quad{\text{as a filtered $K$-module}}.

Our main result is the following:

Theorem 9.3
.

Conjecture
9.1
holds for smooth dagger varieties over
K
with de Rham slopes
\geq 0
.

9.1.1.
Dagger affinoids

The case of affinoids is included in the case of quasi-compact varieties but the proof
is considerably simpler.
We note that it suffices to show that we have natural
isomorphisms since the weak topology on the Hom-spaces is Hausdorff.
Let

M:=H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{C}),\quad X_{\operatorname{st}}^{i}(M):=(M\widehat{\otimes}^{R}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{i}},\quad M_{K}=(\overline{K}\otimes_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}M)^{{\mathscr{G}}_{K}}\simeq H^{i}_{{\rm dR}}(X).

The last isomorphism follows from the Hyodo-Kato isomorphism
[
18
, Th. 4.27]
.
Recall that we have the exact sequence (see Theorem
6.14
)

(9.4)

0\to(\Omega_{X}^{i-1}/\operatorname{Ker}d)\widehat{\otimes}_{K}C\to H^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(i))\to X_{\operatorname{st}}^{i}(M)\to 0.

Applying
{\rm{Hom}}_{{\mathscr{G}}_{K}}(-,{\bf B}_{\operatorname{st}})
and
{\rm{Hom}}_{{\mathscr{G}}_{K}}(-,{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}})
to it, we get the following exact sequences

\displaystyle 0\to

\displaystyle{\rm{Hom}}^{\rm sm}_{{\mathscr{G}}_{K}}(X_{\rm st}^{i}(M),{\bf B}_{\operatorname{st}})\to{\rm{Hom}}^{\rm sm}_{{\mathscr{G}}_{K}}(H^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(i)),{\bf B}_{\operatorname{st}})\to{\rm{Hom}}^{\rm sm}_{{\mathscr{G}}_{K}}((\Omega^{i-1}_{X}/\operatorname{Ker}\,d)\widehat{\otimes}_{K}C,{\bf B}_{\operatorname{st}}),

\displaystyle 0\to

\displaystyle{\rm{Hom}}_{{\mathscr{G}}_{K}}(X_{\rm st}^{i}(M),{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}})\to{\rm{Hom}}_{{\mathscr{G}}_{K}}(H^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(i)),{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}})\to{\rm{Hom}}_{{\mathscr{G}}_{K}}((\Omega^{i-1}_{X}/\operatorname{Ker}\,d)\widehat{\otimes}_{K}C,{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}).

Lemma 9.5
.

We have

{\rm{Hom}}^{\rm sm}_{{\mathscr{G}}_{K}}((\Omega^{i-1}_{X}/\operatorname{Ker}\,d)\widehat{\otimes}_{K}C,{\bf B}_{\operatorname{st}})=0,\quad{\rm{Hom}}_{{\mathscr{G}}_{K}}((\Omega^{i-1}_{X}/\operatorname{Ker}\,d)\widehat{\otimes}_{K}C,{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}})=0.

Proof.

Since
{\rm{Hom}}^{\rm sm}_{{\mathscr{G}}_{K}}((\Omega^{i-1}_{X}/\operatorname{Ker}\,d)\widehat{\otimes}_{K}C,{\bf B}_{\operatorname{st}})\simeq\operatorname{colim}_{L/K}{\rm{Hom}}_{{\mathscr{G}}_{L}}((\Omega^{i-1}_{X}/\operatorname{Ker}\,d)\widehat{\otimes}_{K}C,{\bf B}_{\operatorname{st}})
and

{\bf B}_{\operatorname{st}}\hookrightarrow{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}
, it suffices to show that

{\rm{Hom}}_{{\mathscr{G}}_{L}}((\Omega^{i-1}_{X}/\operatorname{Ker}\,d)\widehat{\otimes}_{K}C,{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}})=0
, for a finite extension
L
of
K
.

But

{\rm{Hom}}_{{\mathscr{G}}_{L}}(C,{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}})=0
(Proposition
2.14
(iv));
hence

{\rm{Hom}}_{{\mathscr{G}}_{L}}((\Omega^{i-1}_{X}/\operatorname{Ker}\,d)\otimes_{K}C,{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}})=0
.
Since our maps are requested to be continuous and

(\Omega^{i-1}_{X}/\operatorname{Ker}\,d)\otimes_{K}C
is dense in
(\Omega^{i-1}_{X}/\operatorname{Ker}\,d)\widehat{\otimes}_{K}C
,
we get the wanted vanishing.
∎

This lemma yields isomorphisms

\displaystyle{\rm{Hom}}^{\rm sm}_{{\mathscr{G}}_{K}}(X_{\rm st}^{i}(M),{\bf B}_{\operatorname{st}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm{Hom}}^{\rm sm}_{{\mathscr{G}}_{K}}(H^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(i)),{\bf B}_{\operatorname{st}}),

\displaystyle{\rm{Hom}}_{{\mathscr{G}}_{K}}(X_{\rm st}^{i}(M),{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm{Hom}}_{{\mathscr{G}}_{K}}(H^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(i)),{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}).

The filtration on
M_{K}
is concentrated in degree
i

(i.e.,
{\rm Fil}^{i}M_{K}=M_{K}
and
{\rm Fil}^{i+1}M_{K}=0
). Hence we can use Example
5.11

of Corollary
5.10
to finish the proof of Conjecture
9.1

in the case of dagger affinoids.

9.1.2.
Quasi-compact dagger varieties with de Rham slopes
\geq 0

Let
X
be a quasi-compact smooth dagger variety over
K
.
Fix
r\geq i
. Set:

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}^{r,i}:=\widetilde{H}^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)),\quad\widetilde{F}^{r,i}:=\widetilde{H}^{i}(F^{r}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{K}{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+})),

\displaystyle{X}^{r,i}:=(H^{i}_{\rm HK}(X_{C})\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\bf B}_{{\rm st}}^{+})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}},\quad B^{i}:=H^{i}_{\rm dR}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{K}{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}.

Let
\widetilde{A}^{r,i}
be the kernel of the canonical map
\widetilde{F}^{r,i}\to B^{i}
. Then

\widetilde{A}^{r,i}
is
also canonically a subgroup of
\widetilde{H}^{r,i}
.
Let

\overline{H}^{r,i}:=\widetilde{H}^{r,i}/\widetilde{A}^{r,i},\quad\overline{F}^{r,i}:=\widetilde{F}^{r,i}/\widetilde{A}^{r,i}.

Note that
\widetilde{F}^{r,i}/\widetilde{A}^{r,i}
is a subgroup of
B^{i}
, hence it is classical.

Lemma 9.6
.

We have, for all
i\leq r
,

{\rm Hom}_{{\mathscr{G}}_{K}}^{\rm sm}(\widetilde{A}^{r,i},{\bf B}_{{\rm st}})=0,\quad{\rm Hom}_{{\mathscr{G}}_{K}}(\widetilde{A}^{r,i},{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}})=0.

Proof.

It suffices to show that

{\rm Hom}_{{\mathscr{G}}_{K}}^{\rm sm}(A^{r,i},{\bf B}_{{\rm st}})=0,\quad{\rm Hom}_{{\mathscr{G}}_{K}}(A^{r,i},{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}})=0.

And, for that, it is enough to prove the second statement.
Let

{\rm DR}^{r,i}:=H^{i}(({\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}\widehat{\otimes}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X))/F^{r})

so that we have a long exact sequence

\cdots\to{\rm DR}^{r,i-1}\to F^{r,i}\to B^{i}\to{\rm DR}^{r,i}\to\cdots
which shows that

A^{r,i}
is a quotient of
{\rm DR}^{r,i-1}
. Hence it is enough
to prove the same statement for
{\rm DR}^{r,i}
, with
i<r
.

Now
{\rm DR}^{r,i}
is the
i
-th hypercohomology group of the complex

{\rm DR}^{r,\bullet}:=\big(({\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}/t^{r})\widehat{\otimes}_{K}{\mathscr{O}}_{X}\to({\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}/t^{r-1})\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\Omega^{1}_{X}\to\cdots\to({\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}/t)\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\Omega^{r-1}_{X}\big).

Choose a covering of
X
by dagger affinoids, and
denote by
Z^{r,i}
the group of
i
-cocycles of the Čech double complex associated
to this covering. Since
{\rm DR}^{r,i}
is a quotient of
Z^{r,i}
, it is enough to prove
that
{\rm Hom}_{{\mathscr{G}}_{K}}(Z^{r,i},{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}})=0
.

Denote by
Z^{j,i}_{K}
the group of
i
-cocycles of the Čech double complex associated
to the above covering and the complex

{\rm DR}^{j,\bullet}_{K}:=\big({\mathscr{O}}_{X}\to\Omega^{1}_{X}\to\cdots\to\Omega^{j-1}_{X}\big).

We are going to prove that
\sum_{j\leq r}(t^{r-j}{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}/t^{r}{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+})\otimes_{K}Z^{j,i}_{K}\to Z^{r,i}
has dense image. This will allow us to conclude since

{\rm Hom}_{{\mathscr{G}}_{K}}((t^{r-j}{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}/t^{r}{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+})\otimes_{K}Z^{j,i}_{K},{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}})=0
by Proposition
2.14

and our maps are assumed to be continuous.

To prove this density, choose a Banach basis over
K
of the
K
-Banach
{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}/t^{r}
of the form

(t^{j}e_{n})_{0\leq j\leq r-1,\,n\in{\bf N}}
(pick a family
e_{n}
of elements of
{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}/t^{r}

whose images in
{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}/t=C
form a Banach basis of
C
over
K
). Then one can use

(t^{j}e_{n})_{0\leq j\leq k-1,\,n\in{\bf N}}
as a Banach basis of
{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}/t^{k}
, if
k\leq r
.
This makes it possible to decompose
{\rm DR}^{r,\bullet}
as a completed direct
sum of the complexes
t^{j}e_{n}\otimes{\rm DR}^{r-j,\bullet}_{K}
’s, and then
Z^{r,i}
is the completion
of the sum of the
t^{j}e_{n}\otimes Z^{r-j,i}_{K}
’s.
∎

It follows from Lemma
9.6
that
we have isomorphisms

{\rm Hom}^{\rm sm}_{{\mathscr{G}}_{K}}(\overline{H}^{r,i},{\bf B}_{{\rm st}})\overset{\sim}{\to}{\rm Hom}_{{\mathscr{G}}_{K}}^{\rm sm}(\widetilde{H}^{r,i},{\bf B}_{{\rm st}}),\quad{\rm Hom}_{{\mathscr{G}}_{K}}(\overline{H}^{r,i},{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}})\overset{\sim}{\to}{\rm Hom}_{{\mathscr{G}}_{K}}(\widetilde{H}^{r,i},{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}})

Since
X
is quasi-compact for which
Conjecture
6.21
holds and
we have the exact sequence (
6.22
):

0\to\widetilde{H}^{r,i}\to X^{r,i}\oplus\widetilde{F}^{r,i}\to B^{i}\to 0.

This induces
exact sequences

(9.7)

0\to\overline{H}^{r,i}\to X^{r,i}\oplus\overline{F}^{r,i}\to B^{i}\to 0,\quad 0\to\overline{H}^{r,i}\to X^{r,i}\to B^{i}/\overline{F}^{r,i}\to 0,\quad

Since
\overline{F}^{r,i}
is a subgroup of
B^{i}
, all the terms in these sequences are classical.
This identifies topologically
\overline{H}^{r,i}
with
V_{\rm st}^{r}(H^{i}_{\rm HK}(X_{C}),H^{i}_{\rm dR}(X))
.
Hence we can use Corollary
5.10
to finish the proof of Conjecture
9.1

in the case of quasi-compact smooth dagger varieties with de Rham slopes
\geq 0
.

9.1.3.
Other small varieties with de Rham slopes
\geq 0

The proof in the case of other small varieties with de Rham slopes
\geq 0

is the same as in the quasi-compact case
using the fact that Conjecture
6.21
holds in that case.

9.2.
The pro-étale–to–de Rham
C_{\rm st}
-conjecture for varieties over
C

The following theorem shows that one can recover de Rham cohomology (without the Hodge filtration)
and Hyodo-Kato cohomology (without actions of
\varphi
and
N
) from
pro-étale cohomology for varieties over
C
, despite the absence of Galois action.

Theorem 9.8
.

Let
X
be a smooth dagger variety over
C
with de Rham slopes
\geq 0
. Let
i\geq 0
.

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}_{\rm TVS}({\mathbb{H}}^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}),{\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm st}})\simeq{\rm Hom}_{F^{\rm nr}}(H^{i}_{\rm HK}(X),{\bf B}_{{\rm st}}),\quad{\text{as a ${\bf B}_{{\rm st}}$-module}},

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}_{\rm TVS}({\mathbb{H}}^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}),{\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm dR}})\simeq{\rm Hom}_{{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}}(H^{i}_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}),{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}),\quad{\text{as a ${\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}$-module}}.

Proof.

If
M
is a qBC or, more generally, a Topological Pair, we set

h(M):={\rm Hom}_{\rm TVS}(M,{\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm dR}}).

Fix
r\geq i
. Set:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{H}}^{r,i}:={\mathbb{H}}^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)),\quad{\mathbb{F}}^{r,i}:=H^{i}(F^{r}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+})\widehat{\otimes}_{{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}}{\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm dR}}^{+})),

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}}^{r,i}:=(H^{i}_{\rm HK}(X_{C})\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm st}}^{+})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}},\quad{\mathbb{B}}^{i}:=H^{i}_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+})\widehat{\otimes}_{{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}}{\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}.

Let
{\mathbb{A}}^{r,i}
be the kernel of the canonical map
{\mathbb{F}}^{r,i}\to{\mathbb{B}}^{i}
. Then

{\mathbb{A}}^{r,i}
is a
{\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}
-Pair; it is
also canonically a subgroup of
{\mathbb{H}}^{r,i}
.
Let

\overline{\mathbb{H}}^{r,i}:={\mathbb{H}}^{r,i}/{\mathbb{A}}^{r,i},\quad\overline{\mathbb{F}}^{r,i}:={\mathbb{F}}^{r,i}/{\mathbb{A}}^{r,i}.

Since
{\mathbb{A}}^{r,i}
is a
{\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}
-Pair, it contains
a dense submodule which is an inductive limit of finite length
{\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}
-Modules,
hence

h({\mathbb{A}}^{r,i})=0
by Corollary
3.18
, and
we have an isomorphism

h(\overline{\mathbb{H}}^{r,i})\overset{\sim}{\to}h({\mathbb{H}}^{r,i}).

We also have natural sequences

\displaystyle 0\to{\mathbb{H}}^{r,i}\to{\mathbb{X}}^{r,i}\oplus{\mathbb{F}}^{r,i}\to{\mathbb{B}}^{i}\to 0,\quad

\displaystyle 0\to{\mathbb{H}}^{r,i}\to{\mathbb{X}}^{r,i}\oplus{\mathbb{F}}^{r,i}\to{\mathbb{B}}^{i}\to 0

\displaystyle 0\to\overline{\mathbb{H}}^{r,i}\to{\mathbb{X}}^{r,i}

\displaystyle\to{\mathbb{B}}^{i}/\overline{\mathbb{F}}^{r,i}\to 0

Since
X
is small, the last sequence is a sequence of BC’s. It is
exact because passing to
C
-points yields the sequence

0\to\overline{H}^{r,i}\to{X}^{r,i}\to{B}^{i}/\overline{F}^{r,i}\to 0
which was proven to be exact,
as a consequence of the validity of
Conjecture
6.21
(see (
9.7
)).
This identifies
\overline{\mathbb{H}}^{r,i}
with

{\mathbb{V}}^{r}_{\rm st}(H^{i}_{\rm HK}(X),t^{-r}\overline{F}^{r,i})
,
which makes it possible to use Proposition
5.20
to prove
Theorem
9.8
.
∎

Remark 9.9
.

(i) If
X
is proper, then
{\mathbb{H}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}^{i}(X,{\bf Q}_{p})

is a finite dimensional
{\bf Q}_{p}
-vector space with
no extra structure. This shows that it is hopeless to try to recover the actions of
\varphi

and
N
on
H^{i}_{\rm HK}(X)
or the filtration on
H^{i}_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+})
using
Theorem
9.8
.

(ii) On the other hand, if
X
is a dagger affinoid, we know what the filtration
on
H^{i}_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+})
is: we have
F^{i+k}H^{i}_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+})=t^{k}H^{i}_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+})
.
Also, as we have seen

(9.10)

(H^{i}_{\rm HK}(X)\otimes_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm st}}^{+})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{i}}\simeq(H^{i}_{\rm HK}(X)\otimes_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm cris}}^{+})^{\varphi=p^{i}}.

Using Lemma
5.17
, one sees that

\displaystyle H^{i}_{\rm HK}(X)^{\boldsymbol{*}}\otimes_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\bf B}_{{\rm cris}}^{+}

\displaystyle\subset{\rm Hom}_{\rm TVS}((H^{i}_{\rm HK}(X)\otimes_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm cris}}^{+})^{\varphi=p^{i}},{\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm cris}}^{+})

\displaystyle\subset\{\lambda\in H^{i}_{\rm HK}(X)^{\boldsymbol{*}}\otimes_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\bf B}_{{\rm cris}},\ \varphi^{n}(\lambda)\in H^{i}_{\rm HK}(X)^{\boldsymbol{*}}\otimes_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+},\ \forall n\geq 0\}

\displaystyle=H^{i}_{\rm HK}(X)^{\boldsymbol{*}}\otimes_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\bf B}_{{\rm cris}}^{+}.

Since, by the proof of Theorem
9.8
,

{\rm Hom}_{\rm TVS}({\mathbb{H}}^{i}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(i)),{\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm cris}}^{+})\simeq{\rm Hom}_{\rm TVS}((H^{i}_{\rm HK}(X)\otimes_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm cris}}^{+})^{\varphi=p^{i}},{\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm cris}}^{+}),

one sees that one
can recover the action of
\varphi
on
