H^{i}_{\rm HK}(X)\otimes_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\breve{C}

(by tensoring with
\breve{C}
above
{\bf B}_{{\rm cris}}^{+}
and dualizing).

(iii) If
X
is a general smooth dagger variety over
C
, point (ii) suggests
that one might be able to recover a sheafified version of the actions
of
\varphi
on
H^{i}_{\rm HK}(X)
and of the filtration on
H^{i}_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}_{{\rm dR}}^{+})
.
Recovering the action of
N
seems out of reach by these methods.

Appendix A
Extensions of topological vector spaces

We list here few basic facts concerning extensions of Banach and Fréchet spaces for which we could not find references.

Proposition A.1
.

Let
0\to W_{1}\to W\to W_{2}\to 0
be an exact sequence in
{\rm LH}(C_{K})
.
If
W_{1},W_{2}
are banachs, so is
W
.

Proof.

Let
U
be an open lattice in
W
, small enough so that
U\cap W_{1}\subset W_{1}^{+}
(this exists
since any open lattice in
W_{1}
is the trace of an open lattice in
W
by strictness of the sequence).
Then the image of
U
in
W_{2}
is open (by strictness of the sequence), hence contains

p^{n}W_{2}^{+}
for
n
big enough. Changing
U
into its intersection with the preimage of
p^{n}W_{2}^{+}
,
we can assume that this image is
p^{n}W_{2}^{+}
, and changing
U
into
U+W_{1}^{+}
, we can assume
that we have an exact sequence
0\to W_{1}^{+}\to U\to p^{n}W_{2}^{+}\to 0
. Let
W^{\prime}
be the banach obtained
by completing
W
for the norm induced by
U
; hence
W^{\prime}=\varprojlim_{n}W/p^{n}U
, and we have a
strict exact sequence
0\to W_{1}\to W^{\prime}\to W_{2}\to 0
as well as a continuous morphism

W\to W^{\prime}
which makes the diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{W_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{W\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{W_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{W_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{W^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{W_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

commutative. We conclude by the
5
Lemma that the middle map is an isomorphism
in
{\rm LH}(C_{K})
hence
W
is a banach.
∎

Remark A.2
.

If
K
is spherically complete or if
W_{1},W_{2}
are separable (i.e. have a
dense subspace of countable dimension) then the above sequence splits.
It seems, on the other hand, that if
\widehat{{\bf C}_{p}}
is a spherical completion
of
{\bf C}_{p}
, the sequence of
{\bf C}_{p}
-banachs

0\to{\bf C}_{p}\to\widehat{{\bf C}_{p}}\to\widehat{{\bf C}_{p}}/{\bf C}_{p}\to 0

does not split. In particular the
{\bf C}_{p}
-dual of
\widehat{{\bf C}_{p}}
is
0
.

Proposition A.3
.

Let
0\to W_{1}\to W\to W_{2}\to 0
be an exact sequence in
{\rm LH}(C_{K})
.
If
W_{1},W_{2}
are fréchets, so is
W
.

Proof.

If
F
is a fréchet and
(U_{n})_{n}
is a basis of neighborhoods of
0
consisting
of open lattices with
U_{n+1}\subset U_{n}
(we can always achieve that by replacing

U_{n}
by
U_{0}\cap U_{1}\cap\dots\cap U_{n}
), then the natural map

F\to\varprojlim_{n}F/U_{n}
is an isomorphism.

Now, for any open lattices
U_{1}\subset W_{1}
and
U_{2}\subset W_{2}
we can construct, as above,
an open lattice
U\subset W
with
U\cap W_{1}\subset U_{1}
and the image of
U
in
W_{2}

contained in
U_{2}
. This makes it possible to construct
a family of neighborhoods
of
0
in
W
consisting
of open lattices with
U_{n+1}\subset U_{n}
, such that the family
(U_{2,n})
of images
of the
U_{n}
are a basis of neighborhoods of
0
in
U_{2}
, and the
U_{1,n}:=W_{1}\cap U_{n}

are a basis of neighborhoods of
0
in
U_{1}
. Let
W^{\prime}:=\varprojlim_{n}W/U_{n}
.
Then
W^{\prime}
is a fréchet (can define the topology by the distance
d(x,y)=p^{-n}

where
n
is the smallest integer with
x-y\notin U_{n}
),
and we have an exact sequence

0\to\varprojlim_{n}W_{1}/U_{1,n}\to W^{\prime}\to\varprojlim_{n}W_{2}/U_{2,n}\to 0
of fréchets
(with the two projective limits being isomorphic to
W_{1}
and
W_{2}
by the above discussion)
as well as a continuous morphism

W\to W^{\prime}
which makes the diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{W_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{W\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{W_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{W_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{W^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{W_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

commutative in
{\rm LH}(C_{K})
It follows that
W\to W^{\prime}
is an isomorphism
in
{\rm LH}(C_{K})
hence
W
is a fréchet.
∎

Proposition A.4
.

Let
0\to W_{1}\to W\to W_{2}\to 0
be an exact sequence in
{\rm LH}(C_{K})
.
If
W_{1},W_{2}
are of compact type and
K
is spherically closed or

W_{1},W_{2}
are separable, so is
W
.

Proof.

Passing to stereotypical duals, we get an exact sequence in
{\rm LH}(C_{K})

0\to W_{2}^{\boldsymbol{*}}\to W^{\boldsymbol{*}}\to W_{1}^{\boldsymbol{*}}\to{\rm Ext}^{1}(W_{2},K)\to\cdots

But
{\rm Ext}^{1}(W_{2},K)=0
since
W_{2}
is separated (see
[
14
, Lemma 2.5]
).
Since
W_{1}^{*},W_{2}^{*}
are nuclear fréchets,
W^{*}
is a fréchet by Proposition
A.3
,
and it is nuclear by
[
19
, Prop. 3.8]
.
Dualizing back, and denoting by
W^{\prime},W^{\prime}_{1},W^{\prime}_{2}
the stereotypical duals
of
W^{\boldsymbol{*}},W_{1}^{\boldsymbol{*}},W_{2}^{\boldsymbol{*}}
, we get a diagram

(A.5)

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 5.5pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-5.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{W_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 56.67496pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.3861pt\raise-9.01941pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\wr}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern which is commutative in
{\rm LH}(C_{K})
where the left and right vertical arrows are isomorphisms
since
W_{1},W_{2}
are of compact type and we have assumed that
K
is spherically
complete or
W_{1},W_{2}
are separable (otherwise we could have
W^{\prime}_{i}=0
…). Hence the rows in diagram (
A.5
) are exact.
It follows that the map
W\to W^{\prime}
is an isomorphism

{\rm LH}(C_{K})
hence
W
is of compact type.
∎

Remark A.6
.

The sequences in Propositions
A.3
and
A.4
have no reason to split,
even if
K
is a finite extension of
{\bf Q}_{p}
.
For example, if
{\rm LA}({\bf Z}_{p})
and
{\rm LC}({\bf Z}_{p})

are the spaces of locally analytic (resp. locally constant) functions

{\bf Z}_{p}\to K
, the derivation
\frac{d}{dx}:{\rm LA}({\bf Z}_{p})\to{\rm LA}({\bf Z}_{p})
is surjective
but Kohlhaase showed in
[
36
, Cor. 4.3]
that it does not have a continuous section (no continuous
primitivation), i.e.,
the sequence of spaces of compact type

0\to{\rm LC}({\bf Z}_{p})\to{\rm LA}({\bf Z}_{p})\to{\rm LA}({\bf Z}_{p})\to 0

does not split.

By duality, this says that if
{\mathscr{R}}^{+}
denotes the ring of analytic functions
on the open unit ball (dual to
{\rm LA}({\bf Z}_{p})
via Amice transform),
and
t=\log(1+T)
, then the sequence
of fréchets

0\to t{\mathscr{R}}^{+}\to{\mathscr{R}}^{+}\to{\mathscr{R}}^{+}/t\to 0

does not split.
